  3. Pass signIn={() => new Promise(() => {})}, which never resolves; type an email and click the button; then expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Signing in…" })).toBeDisabled(). The test finishes while the request is still “running”.
  4. One test per action: added appends a task with done: false; toggled flips one task and leaves the others alone; cleared removes only finished tasks. For immutability, freeze the input with Object.freeze and deep-freeze its tasks, then call the reducer: any attempt to change them throws in strict mode, and expect(next).not.toBe(before) checks a new array came back.
  5. Serve the checkout page as in this chapter; then: select the city, fill the street and phone, click “Next”, choose card, click “Next”, check the summary text with toContainText, click “Place order”, and expect the “Order placed!” status. Run with --trace on and open the trace from test-results with npx playwright show-trace.

Chapter 13

  1. Without defer, LCP grows by about the time the script takes to run on the slowed CPU, because nothing is drawn until it finishes. Without width and height, CLS can grow if the picture’s size becomes known only after the first paint; with this small, quick image it may stay near zero, which is exactly why dimensions are a rule, not a case-by-case decision.
  2. Typical suggestions: “Reduce unused JavaScript”, “Serve images in next-gen formats”, “Eliminate render-blocking resources”, “Avoid an excessive DOM size” and “Reduce the impact of third-party code”. Each one maps to a row of the table in section 13.3.
  3. import { onCLS, onINP, onLCP } from "web-vitals"; onLCP(console.log); onINP(console.log); onCLS(console.log);. In production, replace console.log with a function that sends the metric with navigator.sendBeacon("/vitals", JSON.stringify(metric)), which survives the page being closed.
  4. Collect three results in an array, sort them by LCP, and take the middle one: const runs = []; for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) runs.push(await measure(page)); runs.sort((a, b) => a.lcp - b.lcp); const median = runs[1]!;.
  5. After printing each page, if (lcp > 2500 || cls > 0.1) failed = true;, and at the end process.exitCode = failed ? 1 : 0;. With the pages of this chapter, slow.html fails on LCP and fast.html passes.

Chapter 14

  1. With innerHTML, <svg onload="window.hacked=true"> runs, and so does <iframe src="javascript:…"> in many browsers; any element with an event-handler attribute is a door. With textContent, none of them runs. The lesson: there are far too many tricks to block one by one.
  2. Session cookies of well-run sites are usually HttpOnly, Secure and SameSite=Lax or Strict. Analytics and preference cookies are often readable by JavaScript, which is fine as long as they carry no secrets.
  3. In report-only mode, the console still reports the violation, but the inline script runs: “script ran?” becomes yes. That is why the mode exists: you can see everything a policy would block on a live site, fix it, and only then enforce it.
  4. function safeUrl(input: string): string { try { const url = new URL(input); return url.protocol === "https:" || url.protocol === "http:" ? url.href : "#"; } catch { return "#"; } }. Test a normal https link, an http link, javascript:alert(1), JavaScript:alert(1) (upper case), and a string that is not a URL at all.
  5. An advisory names the package, the affected and fixed versions, a severity, and how the bug can be exploited. Often the vulnerable function is in a build tool that never reaches users, or in code you never call; the right response may be an upgrade, or a note explaining why it does not apply.

Chapter 15

  1. fr-FR groups digits with a narrow space and uses a comma for decimals; hi-IN groups the Indian way, in lakhs and crores (12,34,567.5); fa-IR uses Persian digits, which look slightly different from Arabic-Indic ones.
  2. new Intl.DateTimeFormat("ar-SA-u-ca-gregory", { dateStyle: "full" }) and new Intl.DateTimeFormat("ar-SA-u-ca-islamic-umalqura", { dateStyle: "full" }), then the same with en-US. The calendar extension changes the calendar; the language part only changes the words.
  3. English: { one: "Arrives in {count} day", other: "Arrives in {count} days" }. Arabic: { zero: "يصل اليوم", one: "يصل خلال يوم", two: "يصل خلال يومين", few: "يصل خلال {count} أيام", many: "يصل خلال {count} يوماً", other: "يصل خلال {count} يوم" }. Decide with your team what “zero days” should say; that is a product decision, not a grammar rule.
  4. Typical replacements: margin-left → margin-inline-start, padding-right → padding-inline-end, left: 0 → inset-inline-start: 0, text-align: left → text-align: start, border-left → border-inline-start. Leave genuinely physical things, such as a shadow’s offset, as they are.
  5. The English comment is left-to-right, the Arabic one right-to-left, and the one starting with a number takes the direction of the first strong letter after it, because digits are “weak” in the bidirectional algorithm and do not decide the direction.

Chapter 16

  1. A type-based tree has folders like components/, hooks/ and services/; the feature version has features/cart, features/catalog and so on, each holding its own components, hooks and API calls, plus a small shared/ for truly generic code.
  2. In AccountPage.vue import CartBadge from "../cart". For AppHeader.vue, the real fix is a design decision: either the header stops knowing about the cart and receives the badge as a prop or slot from app/, or it moves into a feature. Shared code must not reach into a feature at all.
  3. Add if (from.layer === "features" && to.layer === "app") (already covered by “nothing imports from app”, so the test proves it), and if (relative(root, file).startsWith("shared/ui") && relative(root, resolve(dirname(file), target)).startsWith("shared/api")) with its own message.
  4. A good ADR is short: one paragraph of context (the problem and the forces), one of decision (what you chose), and a few lines of consequences, good and bad. Number it and never edit an accepted ADR: write a new one that supersedes it.
  5. In eslint.config.js, for files in src/shared/**, add "no-restricted-imports": ["error", { patterns: ["**/features/**"] }]; for src/features/**, a pattern such as "../*/!(index)" or a group for each other feature’s internal paths. ESLint then reports the same two imports in the editor, as you type.

Chapter 18

  1. “nit: data does not say what it holds; unpaidOrders would make the filter below obvious.” “question: what is this check for? If it guards against an old bug, a comment with the issue number would help the next reader.” “issue (blocking): the new branch for refunds has no test, so nothing would tell us if it broke. Could we add one for a refund larger than the order?”
  2. Add if (/\bdebugger\b/.test(code)) say("debugger left in: remove it"); next to the other rules. With a line + debugger; added inside the discount.ts part of pr.diff (and the @@ count increased by one), the robot reports it with its line number, and one more finding in the total.
  3. For each changed file that is code, build its test name with f.replace(/\.ts$/, ".test.ts"), and report “code changed, but its test did not” for each one whose test name is not in changedFiles. Now a change to cart.test.ts no longer excuses an untested discount.ts.
  4. Playwright 79, Cypress 77, manual 41: the winner is unchanged. The sensitivity check still shows Cypress winning if learning counts twice (88 against 92), and doubling “Community and docs” now gives a tie (87 each), which the sort leaves to Playwright, the first option in the list. A tie is a sign to look for another criterion, not to trust the order of the list.
  5. A good record has a context a newcomer understands without asking (the problem, the date, the constraints), at least two real alternatives with why each lost, the decision in one sentence, and honest consequences, including what would make the team look again.
